## Coffin-Breaking Pill
<note>original: *Poguan Dan*; the name implies the formula is powerful enough to revive a person who is already prepared for burial.</note>

The Coffin-Breaking Pill treats **Yin Cold Reversal** <term>Yinjue</term> <note>A critical state in traditional medicine where the body’s vital heat (Yang) is almost entirely extinguished, leading to a death-like coldness and collapse.</note> where the face and eyes have turned blue-green, the area beneath the heart <note>The epigastrium or upper stomach.</note> is hard, the four limbs are cold, and the pulse is so thready <gloss>Fine</gloss> it is on the verge of vanishing.

| | |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Sulfur** <term>Liuhuang</term> | <gloss>One tael. Boil in ash-free wine for three days and three nights; if the liquid is consumed, add more warm wine. When the days are complete, take it out and grind into a fine powder.</gloss> |
| **Cinnabar** <term>Dansha</term> | <gloss>One tael, ground evenly.</gloss> <note>Cinnabar, or mercury sulfide, was a staple of Daoist alchemy, believed to possess life-extending and transformative properties.</note> |

The ingredients above are to be boiled with wine into a paste and formed into pills the size of a **Gorgon fruit** <note>original: *jitou*; literally "chicken head," referring to the seed of the *Euryale ferox* plant, a common size reference for medicinal pills.</note>. 

For one suffering from this illness, first prepare a clean room and do not let others know. Measure the patient's height and dig a pit in the ground about one foot deep. Use a **clover fire** <note>original: *muxu huo*; likely dried alfalfa or clover, chosen perhaps for its specific burning temperature or aromatic properties.</note> to burn within the pit until it is extremely hot. Pour five pints <note>original: *sheng*; a traditional volume measure.</note> of vinegar into the pit to cause the medicinal steam to rise. Lay clothing and quilts inside to cover the pit. 

Dissolve one pill in wine and administer it to the patient. Afterward, have the patient lie inside the pit and keep them covered. After a short while, when **sweat breaks out** <note>Induced sweating was considered a sign that the "Cold" pathogen was being expelled and the "Yang" energy was returning to the surface.</note>, immediately help the patient out and move them to a **place sheltered from the wind**.

<summary>This page describes the "Coffin-Breaking Pill," an emergency remedy for "Yin Reversal" (a state of near-death collapse), involving the use of sulfur and cinnabar combined with an intensive heat-pit therapy.</summary>
<keywords>Coffin-Breaking Pill, Yin Cold Reversal, Sulfur, Cinnabar, Heat therapy, Resuscitation, Hua Tuo</keywords>

### Three-Raw Ingredient Powder for Reviving from Sudden Death and Treating Excess Yin with Cold Collapse and Incessant Vomiting and Diarrhea

Wild Aconite <term>Caowu</term> <gloss>seven pieces</gloss>
Magnolia Bark <term>Houpo</term> <gloss>one foot</gloss>
Licorice Root <term>Gancao</term> <gloss>three inches; use all ingredients in their raw state</gloss> <note>In traditional pharmacy, these herbs are usually roasted or boiled to reduce toxicity. Using them "raw" (sheng) indicates an emergency need for the most potent, aggressive action to "break through" a state of near-death.</note>

Grind the ingredients listed above into a powder. Take one medium cup of water, one *qian* <note>A traditional unit of weight, approximately 3.75 grams.</note> of the powder, and seven jujube dates; decoct the mixture until seven-tenths of the water remains. Administer this as a dose. In severe cases where the patient cannot swallow, force-feed <note>original: *guan*; to pour medicine into the throat of an unconscious person.</note> it to them.

### For Reviving from Sudden Death

Spring Onion Root <term>Conggen</term> <gloss>two taels</gloss>
Melon Pedicel <term>Guadi</term> <gloss>one *fen*</gloss> <note>The stalk of a muskmelon, used in Chinese medicine to clear the sensory orifices and induce the expulsion of phlegm.</note>
Cloves <term>Dingxiang</term> <gloss>fourteen grains</gloss>

Grind the ingredients listed above into a fine powder. Blow one *yizi* <note>Literally "one character's worth," a tiny measurement referring to the amount of powder that would cover the tip of a small spatula or the stroke of a written character.</note> of the powder into the nostrils: into the left nostril for men and the right for women. In a short moment, the patient will naturally revive. However, if the body is already cold and stiff with the "reversal" <note>original: *jue*; a state where the flow of Qi and blood is blocked, leading to cold limbs and loss of consciousness.</note>, they cannot be brought back to life.

## Formula to Revive Those Who Have Died from Heatstroke but Whose Heart Region is Still Warm

<note>In this context, "died" refers to a state of profound unconsciousness or apparent death caused by extreme heat. For this treatment, have the patient lie flat on their back. Take warm water—heated until it is nearly unbearable to the touch—and pour it continuously over the navel. Next, take the urine of a young boy <note>Commonly used in ancient Chinese medicine as a "channel-ushering" agent, believed to have cooling properties and the ability to clear heat-toxins.</note> mixed with the juice of fresh Rehmannia root <term>Shengdihuang</term> and pour this mixture into the patient. They will naturally revive. It is strictly forbidden to give the patient cold water; provide only warm, boiled water for them to drink.

Take three stones <term>dan</term> <note>A 'dan' is a large unit of dry measure, roughly equivalent to 100 liters in this period, suggesting a substantial amount of material for an environmental or external treatment.</note> of lime <term>shihui</term> and mound it up, leaving the top exposed, until the pile is one foot and five inches thick. ### Formula for Treating Death by Hanging

First, have someone lift the person up and untie the rope. You must not use a knife to cut the rope <note>This prohibition is common in ancient manuals, likely reflecting a ritualistic concern for the integrity of the body's spirit or a practical warning against accidental injury during a frantic rescue.</note>. Support the person in a well-ventilated area and lay them down with their head elevated.
Take the powder of scallion roots <term>cong gen</term> and blow it into both nostrils. Furthermore, have a close relative blow air into the person’s mouth <note>This is a remarkably early description of artificial respiration, specifically mouth-to-mouth resuscitation.</note>.
Wait until the person spits out saliva. Then, take alum powder <term>fanshi</term> and a decoction of cloves <term>dingxiang</term>, mix in one mace-spoon <term>qian bi</term>, and pour it into their mouth.

<margin>
Sophora Fruit Powder <term>Huaizi San</term> treats chronic blood in the stool <note>hematochezia</note> and also treats blood in the urine <note>hematuria</note>.
</margin>

# The Water Clarifying Ointment
### This formula treats the same conditions as mentioned previously.
<note>This refers to the "toxic sores, swellings, and back carbuncles" listed in the preceding section.</note>

| | |
| :--- | :--- |
| Well-Spring Stone <note>original: "Jingquanshi." Likely a type of mineral or stalactite found near springs, used for its cooling properties.</note> | Bletilla Rhizome <gloss>one tael each</gloss> <note>A medicinal orchid root used to reduce swelling and promote tissue regeneration.</note> |
| Fossil Bone <note>original: "Longgu" (Dragon Bone). Ancient animal bones used to calm the spirit and astringe fluids.</note> | Phellodendron Bark <note>A bitter, yellow bark used to clear heat and dry dampness.</note> |
| Turmeric Tuber <gloss>half a tael each</gloss> <note>original: "Yujin." Used to invigorate blood and resolve stasis.</note> | |
| Sunset Muskmallow Flower <gloss>one-tenth of a tael</gloss> <note>original: "Huangshukuihua" (Abelmoschus manihot). Used to reduce inflammation.</note> | |

These six ingredients should be ground together into a fine powder. For each application, take two drams <note>original: "two qian," roughly 7.5 grams.</note> and mix the medicine with one cup of freshly drawn well water. Stir until the mixture is uniform and smooth. Wait for the mixture to settle and clarify; discard the clear water on the surface and spread the remaining sediment onto paper patches. Apply these to the affected area. This treats swollen toxins and back carbuncles <note>Severe, life-threatening abscesses or clusters of boils on the back, often treated with urgency in traditional medicine.</note>.

# The Reawakening Ointment
### For all unpredictable malignant sores that are about to <unclear>claim life</unclear>
<note>The damaged characters likely indicate a critical stage where the infection has become life-threatening.</note>

*   **Arisaema Tuber** <gloss>one piece</gloss> <note>original: "Nanxing." A powerful herb for resolving phlegm and "toxic" masses.</note>
*   **Pinellia Tuber** <gloss>seven pieces</gloss> <note>original: "Banxia."</note>
*   **Croton Seed** <gloss>five pieces, shells removed</gloss> <note>original: "Badou." A very potent purgative and irritant used here to "attack" the toxin.</note>
*   **Musk** <gloss>half a dram</gloss> <note>original: "Shexiang." A highly aromatic substance used to open the orifices and penetrate deep-seated blockages.</note>

<summary>This page details two topical medical formulas, the Water Clarifying Ointment and the Reawakening Ointment, used to treat severe skin infections, carbuncles, and life-threatening "malignant" sores.</summary>
<keywords>Water Clarifying Ointment, Reawakening Ointment, back carbuncles, topical medicine, Bletilla, Fossil Bone, Croton seed, traditional surgery</keywords>

--- Page 155 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the Reawakening Ointment from the previous page and introduces two additional potent formulas: the Thousand-Gold Ointment and the Life-Sustaining Pills. These treatments focus on deep-seated "toxic" skin conditions like malignant sores and fistulas.</meta>

Grind the aforementioned ingredients into a fine powder. Take pig lard collected during the twelfth lunar month <note>original: "Layue." Lard from the winter was favored in ancient pharmacy for its supposed purity and resistance to spoilage.</note> and simmer it into a salve. If the sore is not painful, first use a needle to prick the area until a painful spot is reached. Then, mix the medicinal powder with human breast milk <note>Breast milk was often used as a "harmonizing" medium in high-level medical formulas to reduce the harshness of mineral ingredients.</note> and apply it to the site.

# The Thousand-Gold Ointment
### This ointment is applied to all types of malignant sores, carbuncles, and boils.
<note>The name "Thousand-Gold" (Qianjin) implies that the formula is so valuable it is worth a thousand pieces of gold.</note>

| | |
| :--- | :--- |
| Prepared Lead Powder <note>original: "Dingfen." Historically used to dry dampness and kill parasites.</note> | Southern Lead Powder <note>original: "Nanfen." A variant of white lead.</note> |
| Calomel <note>original: "Nifen" (Greasy Powder). Mercurous chloride, used for severe skin infections but highly toxic.</note> | Minium <note>original: "Huangdan" (Yellow Cinnabar/Red Lead). Used to promote healing and draw out toxins.</note> |

<center>Take one part of each of the above.</center>

Grind the ingredients mentioned above into a fine powder. Add one dram <note>original: "one qian."</note> of musk <note>original: "Shexiang." A highly aromatic substance used to "pierce" through blockages and bring the medicine deep into the flesh.</note> and grind until uniform. Mix with oil to reach the proper consistency for an ointment, then apply. 

<gloss>If the sore is as small as the eye of a needle-cloth, insert the ointment into it. Apply Mica Ointment over the top.</gloss>

# Life-Sustaining Pills
### These treat long-standing or recent malignant symptoms and suppurating fistulas.
<note>original: "Dingming Yuan." Literally "Pills that Fix Fate," used for life-threatening chronic infections that have formed "leaking" tunnels (fistulas) in the flesh.</note>

<gloss>Grind this medicine into powder. Melt wax into hot water and form the mixture into strips while submerged.</gloss>

| | |
| :--- | :--- |
| Realgar <gloss>one-tenth of a tael</gloss> <note>original: "Xionghuang." An arsenic sulfide mineral used to "attack" toxins and parasites.</note> | Frankincense <gloss>one-tenth of a tael</gloss> <note>original: "Ruxiang." Used to move blood and alleviate pain.</note> |
| Croton Seeds <gloss>twenty-one grains</gloss> | <gloss>Remove the skin but do not extract the oil.</gloss> <note>Croton seeds are a powerful, toxic purgative. The oil is the most active and irritating component.</note> |

Grind the above ingredients into a powder as fine as flour. Add three drams <note>original: "three qian."</note> of white wheat flour. Blend with water and roll into pills the size of small beans or wheat grains.

# Carbuncles and Phlegmonous Sores
<note>The term "Yongju" in traditional Chinese medicine refers to serious skin and soft tissue infections. "Yong" are generally acute, red, and swollen abscesses, while "Ju" are deeper, more "toxic" carbuncles that often involve tissue death.</note>

Use large "Two-Headed Sharp" seeds <note>original: "Liangtoujian." These are the rhizomes of *Anemone raddeana*, shaped like tiny spears. They were used to physically probe or "pierce" the stagnation within a wound.</note>. Measure the depth of the illness and insert the medicine into the center of the sore. Apply Frankincense Ointment <note>original: "Ruxianggao."</note> over it for an effective treatment. Taking Mica Ointment <note>original: "Yunmugao." Mica was a prized "stone" medicine believed to purge toxins and assist in the regeneration of healthy flesh.</note> is even better.

### Musk Pills for Treating All Types of Qi-Fistulas
<note>"Leaking sores" (Louchuang) refers to fistulas—narrow, tunnel-like tracks that form when a deep infection constantly drains to the surface.</note>

Musk <note>original: "Shexiang," written here with a variant character.</note> <gloss>one part</gloss>
Frankincense <gloss>one part</gloss>
Croton Seeds <gloss>fourteen pieces, skins removed</gloss> <note>Croton is an extremely potent, "hot" purgative. In this topical context, it was used to chemically debride (eat away) the dead or infected "pipe" of the fistula.</note>

Grind the ingredients listed above into a powder. Mix with the pulp of dates to form a medicinal dough. Shape the dough into small, elongated bars <note>original: "dingzi." These were essentially medicinal suppositories or "tents" designed to be pushed deep into a wound track.</note>. Depending on the depth of the sore, insert the medicine as needed. Apply Frankincense Ointment over the site, continuing until the treatment takes effect.

### Musk-Rat Powder for Treating Leaking Sores

Musk-rat skin <gloss>forty-nine pieces; those with patterned backs found in rivers are the correct variety</gloss>
Dragon Bone <note>original: "Longgu." These are fossilized bones of ancient mammals, used in Chinese medicine for their ability to dry up dampness and close persistent wounds.</note> <gloss>half an ounce</gloss>
Bat <gloss>two pieces; use only the heart and liver</gloss>
Minium <note>original: "Huangdan." Also known as red lead, it was a staple in ancient "drawing salves" used to pull out toxins.</note> <gloss>one part</gloss>

<summary>This page describes specific treatments for "leaking" sores and carbuncles, utilizing inserted medicinal bars (tents) and ointments. It emphasizes the use of potent ingredients like musk, croton, and dragon bone to treat deep-seated infections.</summary>

<keywords>carbuncles, phlegmon, fistulas, musk pills, Frankincense Ointment, two-headed sharp, dragon bone, traditional surgery, Hua Tuo</keywords>

Fire the vessel using three measures of charcoal. Once the firing is complete, let the vessel cool, then grind the contents into a fine powder for use.

The effect will be immediate.

## Formula for Treating White Carbuncles Accompanied by Chills, Wheezing, and Dizziness

<note>original: "Baiding." Literally "White Clove," referring to a deep-seated, hard boil that resembles a clove bud. In this case, the infection has progressed to cause systemic symptoms like respiratory distress and fainting.</note>

Lepidium Seeds, Rhubarb <gloss>one tael of each</gloss> <note>original: "Tingli" and "Dahuang." These are potent purging agents used to drain heat and fluid from the lungs and bowels.</note> Mulberry Bark, Poria <gloss>two taels of each</gloss> <note>original: "Sangbaipi" and "Fuling." Used to regulate fluids and reduce swelling.</note>

Areca Nut <gloss>seven pieces</gloss> Bush Cherry Seed, Stephania Root <gloss>three parts of each</gloss> <note>original: "Binglang," "Yuliren," and "Hanfangji." These ingredients promote the downward movement of "Qi" and fluids to clear toxins through the digestive and urinary tracts.</note>

Grind the items listed above into a fine powder. For each dose, take three mace <note>approximately 11 grams</note> mixed with honey water. The dosage should be adjusted based on the degree to which "evil substances" <note>referring to morbid matter, pus, or toxins</note> are purged from the lower body.

## Another Formula for Extracting White Carbuncles

Lead Frost <gloss>one part</gloss> <note>original: "Qianshuang." An alchemical preparation of lead acetate.</note> Blue Vitriol, Calomel <gloss>one mace of each</gloss> <note>original: "Danfan" and "Fenshuang." These are highly potent mineral compounds (copper sulfate and mercurous chloride) used in ancient medicine to aggressively "draw out" deep-seated toxins.</note> Centipede <gloss>one whole</gloss>

Grind the items listed above into a fine powder. First, prick the sore to draw out blood. Place a portion of the powder (about the size of a grain heart) into the wound. Finally, seal it using a small cake made of vinegar and dough.

# Immediate Healing for the Mouth

## Formula for Treating Infants
<note>original: "Chizi." Literally "red child," a term used for newborns or young infants whose skin still has a reddish hue.</note>

Coptis, Rhubarb <gloss>one tael of each</gloss>
<note>original: "Huanglian" and "Dahuang." Both are extremely bitter herbs used in traditional medicine to "drain fire" and eliminate toxins from the body.</note>

Grind the ingredients above into a powder. Mix with raw honey and form into pills the size of a paulownia seed <note>The seed of the *Paulownia* tree was a standard reference for pill size, roughly 6–8 millimeters in diameter.</note>. For each dose, take thirty pills with warm water. Use the ease of bowel movements as the measure for the dosage <note>original: "yi li wei du." The goal was to ensure the infant's bowels were moving freely to clear the internal heat causing the mouth ailment.</note>.

## Another Formula for Infants

Apricot Kernels <gloss>seven pieces, used raw</gloss>
<note>original: "Xingren." While often used for coughs, raw apricot kernels were also used in topical applications for their oil and mildly sedative properties.</note>

Chew the ingredient above until it is a soft pulp. Use it to rinse the mouth, allowing the saliva to fill the oral cavity, then spit it out. Filter the resulting juice through a fine silk cloth and add a small amount of Calomel <note>original: "Qingfen." Mercurous chloride, a mineral used historically in many cultures as a topical treatment for skin diseases and sores, though its toxicity is now well-known.</note>. Stir until the mixture is even, then use a chicken feather to brush the medicine onto the affected area.

<summary>This page provides two pediatric formulas for mouth ailments: an internal pill-based treatment using Coptis and Rhubarb to purge heat, and a topical wash made from apricot kernels and calomel.</summary>
<keywords>pediatrics, infant care, mouth sores, Coptis, Rhubarb, Calomel, detoxification, traditional pharmacology</keywords>

# Medical Classic of Mouth Carbuncles, Volume II

## Formula for Treating Yellow Carbuncles
<note>original: "Huangding." These are yellowish, pus-filled sores or carbuncles occurring in or around the mouth, often associated with severe internal "damp-heat" in traditional Chinese pathology.</note>

Croton Seeds <gloss>seven pieces, membranes removed</gloss>
<note>original: "Badou." A potent and toxic purgative herb used to aggressively clear blockages. Removing the membrane and roasting (as described below) was a traditional method to reduce its harsh toxicity.</note>
Qingzhou Dates <gloss>seven pieces, pits removed; place the croton seeds inside the dates, wrap in dough, and roast until glowing red</gloss>
<note>original: "Qingzhou zao." A specific variety of jujube fruit from Qingzhou, prized for its medicinal quality.</note>

Grind the ingredients above into a powder. Use Borax <note>original: "Pengsha." A naturally occurring mineral used for its antiseptic and cooling properties.</note> and vinegar to make a dough paste, then form into pills the size of a kidney bean. For each dose:

Take five to ten pills with rice water. Use the ease of bowel movements as the measure for the dosage. <note>original: "yi li wei du." Similar to the previous page, the treatment aims to purge the "poison" through the digestive tract until the bowels move freely.</note>

---

## Another Formula for Treating Yellow Carbuncles

Phellodendron Bark <gloss>two taels</gloss>
<note>original: "Huangbo." Also known as Amur Cork Tree bark; a cold, bitter herb used to clear heat and dry dampness.</note>
Turmeric Root <gloss>half a tael</gloss>
<note>original: "Yujin." The tuber of the turmeric plant, used to cool the blood and resolve swelling.</note>

Grind the ingredients above into a fine powder. Mix with egg white and use a chicken feather to brush the mixture onto the affected area.

---

## Formula for Treating Black Carbuncles
<note>original: "Heiding." Black carbuncles or "clove-sores" were considered more severe and deeply rooted than yellow ones, often indicating necrotic tissue or "blood heat" toxins.</note>

Dodder Seed, Sweetflag Rhizome
<note>original: "Tusizi" and "Changpu." Dodder seed is typically a tonic, while Sweetflag (Acorus) is used for its aromatic properties to open the "orifices" and treat sores.</note>

<summary>This page details aggressive internal and topical treatments for "Yellow Carbuncles" of the mouth, emphasizing the use of processed croton seeds and cooling herbs, and begins the section on "Black Carbuncles."</summary>
<keywords>Yellow Carbuncles, Black Carbuncles, Croton seeds, Phellodendron bark, pediatric medicine, detoxification, purgative therapy</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the treatment for Yellow Carbuncles (begun on the previous page) and introduces a specific protocol for Green Carbuncles, before marking the conclusion of the final volume of the work.</meta>

Grind the two ingredients mentioned above into equal parts. Soak the powder in wine to extract the medicinal juice, then brush this liquid over the carbuncles. Additionally, the patient should take Kidney Qi Pills <note>original: "Shenqi yuan." A foundational formula used to tonify the kidneys and restore the body's "source qi" after it has been weakened by illness or aggressive purging treatments.</note> to replenish their strength.

### Formula for Treating Green Carbuncles
<note>original: "Qingding." These refer to greenish-colored sores or carbuncles. In the five-phase system of traditional medicine, the color green is associated with the Liver; thus, these sores were thought to be caused by deep-seated toxins or "wind-heat" in the Liver and Gallbladder systems.</note>

Pipewort <gloss>one tael</gloss>
<note>original: "Gujingcao." Literally "grain essence grass," this plant is traditionally used to clear heat, especially from the eyes and head.</note>
Cicada Slough <gloss>one tael</gloss>
<note>original: "Chanke." The cast-off skin of the cicada, used in traditional formulas to dispel "wind" from the skin and soothe irritation.</note>
Black Atractylodes <gloss>five taels</gloss>
<note>original: "Cangzhu." A pungent, aromatic root used to dry dampness and strengthen the digestive system.</note>

Grind the ingredients listed above into a fine powder. For each dose, take one *qian* <note>A traditional unit of weight, approximately 3.75 grams.</note> mixed with water. This should be taken before meals. Furthermore, use a medical needle to prick the carbuncle to release the toxic fluid. Afterward, wash the area with a solution made from mulberry wood ash <note>original: "Sangchai huizhi." Lye produced from mulberry ash was a common ancient disinfectant used to "draw out" toxins and cleanse wounds.</note>. This treatment is immediately effective.

## End of the Final Volume of Master Hua’s Central Treasury Medical Classic
